## 1. camera_obj_rel_dist_v1: sb_rowB4_5dcea2bde044c64d11313ab5

Measuring from the closest point of each object, which of these objects (plant, curtain, coffee table, tv stand) is the closest to the camera in frame 6 of 32?

A. plant
B. curtain
C. coffee table
D. tv stand

<think>
Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 6 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "plant" is in view in frame 6; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the curtain.
Step 3: the object labelled "curtain" is in view in frame 6; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the tv stand and nearer than the plant.
Step 4: the object labelled "coffee table" is in view in frame 6; relative to that camera, its closest point is nearer than the tv stand.
Step 5: the object labelled "tv stand" is in view in frame 6; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the coffee table and nearer than the curtain.
Step 6: ordered nearest to farthest from that camera, the objects are coffee table, then tv stand, then curtain, then plant.
So the closest object to that camera is the coffee table.
</think>

C

## 2. camera_obj_rel_dist_v1: sb_rowB4_16e202bcc5e8a3d758fcdfb5

Measuring from the closest point of each object, which of these objects (tv, dishwasher, stove, refrigerator) is the closest to the camera in frame 20 of 32?

A. tv
B. dishwasher
C. stove
D. refrigerator

<think>
Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 20 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "tv" is out of view in frame 20; it was last seen in frame 12; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the stove and nearer than the refrigerator.
Step 3: the object labelled "dishwasher" is out of view in frame 20; it was last seen in frame 10; relative to that camera, its closest point is nearer than the stove.
Step 4: the object labelled "stove" is out of view in frame 20; it was last seen in frame 18; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the dishwasher and nearer than the tv.
Step 5: the object labelled "refrigerator" is out of view in frame 20; it was last seen in frame 17; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the tv.
Step 6: ordered nearest to farthest from that camera, the objects are dishwasher, then stove, then tv, then refrigerator.
So the closest object to that camera is the dishwasher.
</think>

B

## 3. camera_obj_rel_dist_v1: sb_rowB4_c639ad13c5cfa7ead219c2fa

Measuring from the closest point of each object, which of these objects (telephone, table, trash can, tv) is the closest to the camera in frame 6 of 32?

A. telephone
B. table
C. trash can
D. tv

<think>
Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 6 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "telephone" is out of view in frame 6; it was last seen in frame 3; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the trash can and nearer than the tv.
Step 3: the object labelled "table" is in view in frame 6; relative to that camera, its closest point is nearer than the trash can.
Step 4: the object labelled "trash can" is out of view in frame 6; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the table and nearer than the telephone.
Step 5: for the object instance labelled "trash can", it first appears in frame 19, after frame 6.
Step 6: the object labelled "tv" is in view in frame 6; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the telephone.
Step 7: ordered nearest to farthest from that camera, the objects are table, then trash can, then telephone, then tv.
So the closest object to that camera is the table.
</think>

B

## 4. camera_obj_rel_dist_v1: sb_rowB4_d20f648832cd13d94dafa67f

Measuring from the closest point of each object, which of these objects (stool, cabinet, fireplace, tv) is the closest to the camera in frame 3 of 32?

A. stool
B. cabinet
C. fireplace
D. tv

<think>
Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 3 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "stool" is out of view in frame 3; it was last seen in frame 2; relative to that camera, its closest point is nearer than the cabinet.
Step 3: the object labelled "cabinet" is out of view in frame 3; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the stool and nearer than the fireplace.
Step 4: for the object instance labelled "cabinet", it first appears in frame 10, after frame 3.
Step 5: the object labelled "fireplace" is out of view in frame 3; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the cabinet and nearer than the tv.
Step 6: for the object instance labelled "fireplace", it first appears in frame 5, after frame 3.
Step 7: the object labelled "tv" is out of view in frame 3; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the fireplace.
Step 8: for the object instance labelled "tv", it first appears in frame 8, after frame 3.
Step 9: ordered nearest to farthest from that camera, the objects are stool, then cabinet, then fireplace, then tv.
So the closest object to that camera is the stool.
</think>

A

## 5. camera_obj_rel_dist_v1: sb_rowB4_7409f2e247a8635e47a00bca

Measuring from the closest point of each object, which of these objects (door, heater, bookshelf, cloth) is the closest to the camera in frame 28 of 32?

A. door
B. heater
C. bookshelf
D. cloth

<think>
Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 28 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "door" is in view in frame 28; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the cloth.
Step 3: the object labelled "heater" is out of view in frame 28; it was last seen in frame 27; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the bookshelf and nearer than the cloth.
Step 4: the object labelled "bookshelf" is out of view in frame 28; it was last seen in frame 25; relative to that camera, its closest point is nearer than the heater.
Step 5: the object labelled "cloth" is in view in frame 28; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the heater and nearer than the door.
Step 6: ordered nearest to farthest from that camera, the objects are bookshelf, then heater, then cloth, then door.
So the closest object to that camera is the bookshelf.
</think>

C

## 7. camera_obj_rel_dist_v2: sb_rowB4_a28286f1ba62953449ba510d

Measuring from the closest point of each object, which of these objects (bathtub, cabinet, bed) is the closest to the camera in frame 1 of 32?

A. bathtub
B. cabinet
C. bed

<think>
Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 1 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "bathtub" is out of view in frame 1; relative to that camera, its closest point is nearer than the cabinet.
Step 3: for the object instance labelled "bathtub", it first appears in frame 5, after frame 1.
Step 4: the object labelled "cabinet" is out of view in frame 1; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the bathtub and nearer than the bed.
Step 5: for the object instance labelled "cabinet", it first appears in frame 13, after frame 1.
Step 6: the object labelled "bed" is out of view in frame 1; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the cabinet.
Step 7: for the object instance labelled "bed", it first appears in frame 2, after frame 1.
Step 8: ordered nearest to farthest from that camera, the objects are bathtub, then cabinet, then bed.
So the closest object to that camera is the bathtub.
</think>

A

## 8. camera_obj_rel_dist_v2: sb_rowB4_e0bb7106752a8d309aadeab3

Measuring from the closest point of each object, which of these objects (tv, shelf, refrigerator) is the closest to the camera in frame 19 of 32?

A. tv
B. shelf
C. refrigerator

<think>
Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 19 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "tv" is out of view in frame 19; it was last seen in frame 9; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the refrigerator and nearer than the shelf.
Step 3: the object labelled "shelf" is out of view in frame 19; it was last seen in frame 8; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the tv.
Step 4: the object labelled "refrigerator" is in view in frame 19; relative to that camera, its closest point is nearer than the tv.
Step 5: ordered nearest to farthest from that camera, the objects are refrigerator, then tv, then shelf.
So the closest object to that camera is the refrigerator.
</think>

C

## 9. camera_obj_rel_dist_v2: sb_rowB4_8cd6332059dcb6359307a731

Measuring from the closest point of each object, which of these objects (shelf, tv, sofa) is the closest to the camera in frame 2 of 32?

A. shelf
B. tv
C. sofa

<think>
Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 2 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "shelf" is out of view in frame 2; relative to that camera, its closest point is nearer than the tv.
Step 3: for the object instance labelled "shelf", it first appears in frame 5, after frame 2.
Step 4: the object labelled "tv" is out of view in frame 2; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the shelf and nearer than the sofa.
Step 5: for the object instance labelled "tv", it first appears in frame 12, after frame 2.
Step 6: the object labelled "sofa" is out of view in frame 2; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the tv.
Step 7: for the object instance labelled "sofa", it first appears in frame 5, after frame 2.
Step 8: ordered nearest to farthest from that camera, the objects are shelf, then tv, then sofa.
So the closest object to that camera is the shelf.
</think>

A

## 11. camera_obj_rel_dist_v3: sb_rowB4_29d39a358b82bae1414e4568

Measuring from the closest point of each object, which of these objects (tv, chair) is the closest to the camera in frame 9 of 32?

A. tv
B. chair

<think>
Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 9 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "tv" is in view in frame 9; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the chair.
Step 3: the object labelled "chair" is in view in frame 9; relative to that camera, its closest point is nearer than the tv.
Step 4: ordered nearest to farthest from that camera, the objects are chair, then tv.
So the closest object to that camera is the chair.
</think>

B

## 12. camera_obj_rel_dist_v3: sb_rowB4_14fd38e13f8c53bb177974f7

Measuring from the closest point of each object, which of these objects (sofa, chair) is the closest to the camera in frame 1 of 32?

A. sofa
B. chair

<think>
Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 1 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "sofa" is out of view in frame 1; relative to that camera, its closest point is nearer than the chair.
Step 3: for the object instance labelled "sofa", it first appears in frame 13, after frame 1.
Step 4: the object labelled "chair" is out of view in frame 1; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the sofa.
Step 5: for the object instance labelled "chair", it first appears in frame 11, after frame 1.
Step 6: ordered nearest to farthest from that camera, the objects are sofa, then chair.
So the closest object to that camera is the sofa.
</think>

A

## 13. camera_obj_rel_dist_v3: sb_rowB4_eb85ca9abe2460f0ba214230

Measuring from the closest point of each object, which of these objects (box, lamp) is the closest to the camera in frame 3 of 32?

A. box
B. lamp

<think>
Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 3 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "box" is out of view in frame 3; it was last seen in frame 1; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the lamp.
Step 3: the object labelled "lamp" is in view in frame 3; relative to that camera, its closest point is nearer than the box.
Step 4: ordered nearest to farthest from that camera, the objects are lamp, then box.
So the closest object to that camera is the lamp.
</think>

B

A. tv
B. toilet

<think>
Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 28 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "tv" is out of view in frame 28; it was last seen in frame 15; relative to that camera, its closest point is nearer than the toilet.
Step 3: the object labelled "toilet" is out of view in frame 28; it was last seen in frame 5; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the tv.
Step 4: ordered nearest to farthest from that camera, the objects are tv, then toilet.
So the closest object to that camera is the tv.
</think>

A

## 16. obj_obj_relative_pos_lr: sb_rowB4_eee03d5080a07af7f0bced2b

In frame 2 of 32, relative to bathtub, is toilet to the [Left/Right]?

A. Left
B. Right

<think>
Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 2 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "toilet" is in view in frame 2; from that camera viewpoint, its centre is to the left of the centre of the bathtub.
Step 3: the object labelled "bathtub" is in view in frame 2; from that camera viewpoint, its centre is to the right of the centre of the toilet.
So from that camera viewpoint, the centre of the toilet is to the left of the centre of the bathtub.
</think>

A

A. Right
B. Left

Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 1 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "cabinet" is out of view in frame 1; from that camera viewpoint, its centre is to the left of the centre of the bed.
Step 3: for the object instance labelled "cabinet", it first appears in frame 3, after frame 1.
Step 4: the object labelled "bed" is out of view in frame 1; from that camera viewpoint, its centre is to the right of the centre of the cabinet.
Step 5: for the object instance labelled "bed", it first appears in frame 2, after frame 1.
So from that camera viewpoint, the centre of the cabinet is to the left of the centre of the bed.
</think>

B

## 20. obj_obj_relative_pos_lr: sb_rowB4_be302dbd932f442db12cab8e

In frame 28 of 32, relative to refrigerator, is stove to the [Left/Right]?

A. Right
B. Left

<think>
Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 28 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "stove" is out of view in frame 28; it was last seen in frame 26; from that camera viewpoint, its centre is to the right of the centre of the refrigerator.
Step 3: the object labelled "refrigerator" is out of view in frame 28; it was last seen in frame 13; from that camera viewpoint, its centre is to the left of the centre of the stove.
So from that camera viewpoint, the centre of the stove is to the right of the centre of the refrigerator.
</think>

A

## 23. obj_obj_relative_pos_nf: sb_rowB4_31afd5a6e6692c5429b3023e

In frame 29 of 32, relative to the camera, is bathtub [Near/Far] compared to cabinet?

A. Far
B. Near

<think>
Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 29 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "bathtub" is out of view in frame 29; it was last seen in frame 26; relative to that camera, its closest point is farther than the closest point of the cabinet.
Step 3: the object labelled "cabinet" is in view in frame 29; relative to that camera, its closest point is nearer than the closest point of the bathtub.
So relative to that camera, the closest point of the bathtub is farther than the closest point of the cabinet.
</think>

A

In frame 32 of 32, relative to sink, is cabinet to the [Up/Down]?

A. Up
B. Down

<think>
Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 32 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "cabinet" is in view in frame 32; in that camera's level horizon, its centre is lower than the centre of the sink.
Step 3: the object labelled "sink" is in view in frame 32; in that camera's level horizon, its centre is higher than the centre of the cabinet.
So the centre of the cabinet is lower than the centre of the sink in that camera's level horizon.
</think>

B

## 27. obj_obj_relative_pos_ud: sb_rowB4_2f1a5726b0731e07e0379e4f

In frame 19 of 32, relative to shelf, is table to the [Up/Down]?

A. Up
B. Down

<think>
Step 1: take the camera viewpoint of frame 19 of 32. Sighting references use raster visibility.
Step 2: the object labelled "table" is out of view in frame 19; it was last seen in frame 6; in that camera's level horizon, its centre is lower than the centre of the shelf.
Step 3: the object labelled "shelf" is out of view in frame 19; it was last seen in frame 14; in that camera's level horizon, its centre is higher than the centre of the table.
So the centre of the table is lower than the centre of the shelf in that camera's level horizon.
</think>

B

In frame 22 of 32, relative to table, is tv to the [Up/Down]?
